# Kanji Mnemonics Review

Total: 2991 kanji

---

## JLPT N5 (80 kanji)

### 一 (one)

**Story:** One down, 2041 to go. ,).

_By Piitaa (263 votes)_

---

### 二 (two)

**Story:** Roman numeral II, written on its side.

_By mazel (24 votes)_

---

### 三 (three)

**Story:** THREE measurements define the female shape: BUST, WAIST, and HIPS. Waist is the smallest, and for most women the HIPS are wider than the chest.

**Components:** 一 (one)

_By Harrow (49 votes)_

---

### 四 (four)

**Story:** FOUR year-olds often put their FOOT (human legs) in their MOUTH - but they are too young to realize.

**Components:** 儿 (legs) + 囗 (enclosure) + 丿 (slash/stroke)

_By tomb771 (115 votes)_

---

### 五 (five)

**Story:** 三 + two vertical lines = 5.

**Components:** 一 (one) + 二 (two)

_By NooNoo (236 votes)_

---

### 六 (six)

**Story:** Indicating 6 parts of the human body: head, body, 2 arms, 2 legs.

**Components:** 八 (eight) + 亠 (lid)

_By Andre_Breton (127 votes)_

---

### 七 (seven)

**Story:** This kanji is our good old seven , turned upside down.

**Components:** 一 (one) + 乙 (second)

_By munia (243 votes)_

---

### 八 (eight)

**Story:** "Hachi" (eight) begins with the sound of the katakana "ha" , and it's approximately the same character.

_By clemk (120 votes)_

---

### 九 (nine)

**Story:** Kinda of looks like the kanji of 1 一 with the kanji of 8 八 added. So think of it as 一＋八＝九 .

**Components:** 乙 (second) + 丿 (slash/stroke)

_By Gunii (157 votes)_

---

### 十 (ten)

**Story:** Two needles crossing marks the spot, or you can use the Roman character for ten (rotated 45 degrees).

_By Christine_Tham (68 votes)_

---

### 日 (day)

**Story:** Open the window, new day.

_By Alphinux (120 votes)_

---

### 月 (month)

**Story:** A month is longer than a day.

_By cerulean (157 votes)_

---

### 白 (white)

**Story:** A white ray is a drop of sun.

**Components:** 日 (sun; day)

_By Christine_Tham (130 votes)_

**Story:** It takes many drops of ten to get to a thousand.

**Components:** 一 (one)

**Story:** A child who is ten will still ask for his ma (top looks a bit like katakana ma).

_By Christine_Tham (13 votes)_

**Story:** A little penguin with its wings at its side.

_By Harrow (121 votes)_

**Story:** In the evening, when it becomes dark, open your mouth to say your name so that people can recognise you.

**Components:** 口 (mouth) + 夕 (night)

---

### 川 (stream)

**Story:** So as not to confuse this with river (#146 河) think of stream ers falling down.

_By ceeeps (86 votes)_

---

### 水 (water)

**Story:** The kanji for water is an abstract primitive itself, so unless one is willing to break down its etymological development, it should just simply be memorized by strokes, constructing a story is not necessarily useful unless it strongly denotes its shape (I find that Heisig's story doesn't do this).

_By Danieru (67 votes)_

---

### 土 (soil)

**Story:** Remembering soil is easy. If soil is soft, you can take a walking stick and plunge it two* feet into the ground.

_By mparrett1 (87 votes)_

---

**Story:** When the SUN starts to shine on the TEMPLE, it's time to get up.

_By zdnks (120 votes)_

### 話 (tale)

**Story:** Tale s are always spoken, not written, and because of this, words fly out of your tongue , rather than your pen.

_By dicedice (178 votes)_

**Story:** The simplest word to say is I.

**Components:** 言 (say; word) + 五 (five) + 二 (two) + 口 (mouth)

_By rosie (226 votes)_

### 読 (read)

**Story:** People will say ( 言 ) almost anything to make a sale ( 売 ), don't believe everything you read ( 読 ).

**Story:** From the ceiling hangs a towel dripping four drops like RAIN.

### 天 (heavens)

**Story:** If you think of the sky as a ceiling above our heads, here you have the heavens as the largest ceiling you could ever imagine. As Heisig pointed out, nothing comes above the ceiling primitive thus the order of primitives is taken care of.

**Components:** 一 (one) + 大 (big)

_By fuaburisu (123 votes)_

---

### 北 (north)

**Story:** Warning: The left part is NOT the primitive turtle! The stroke order is different. Sometimes it will look exactly the same but not always depending on the font / style used to draw this character. Be sure to study the difference!

**Components:** 匕 (spoon)

_By Codexus (148 votes)_

**Components:** 雨 (rain) + 日 (sun; day)

_By uberclimber (170 votes)_

**Story:** Friend s with benefits anyone?

**Components:** 又 (again  また)

**Story:** YOU CAN'T POSSIBLY NEED A MNEMONIC FOR THIS!!!!

_By the40pancakes (108 votes)_

### 男 (man)

**Story:** Male is seen here as muscles working in the field, in other words working in the rice fields is the work of the male who are usually stronger. This is more or less the meaning of the original Chinese character.

**Components:** 力 (effort) + 田 (rice field)

_By fuaburisu (212 votes)_

---

### 行 (going)

**Story:** To me, this looks like a bridge going over a body of water, being supported by columns underneath it. Being that I live near the ocean, I see tourists and vacationers coming and going constantly on the bridge. Because the bridge is so long, it forms an arc to support the structural integrity. You can see that arc here.

**Components:** 彳 (step)

_By jackhuddleston (76 votes)_

---

### 人 (person)

**Story:** Behold Mister T (of "A-Team" fame)! I chose Mr.T because he's sure to make amusing stories. He is a very versatile character since he's both the big, tough guy and the “moralistic” person , telling kids not “to do drugs or alcohol”. He is easy to imagine, the heavy gold chains are probably the most prominent feature of the character. And last but not least you can always pepper your story with the famous line “I pity da foo!” * Fittingly, on the left side of the kanji, it looks like a “T”.

_By fuaburisu (156 votes)_

---

**Story:** Mr T rest ing by a tree.

**Components:** 木 (tree)

_By synewave (135 votes)_
